Known issues

Everything we know looks or behaves differently from the PC game, or got in the way on the Vita, and whether it is fixed. 46 in all: 9 open · 17 with a fix planned · 20 fixed. Tap one for the details.

  • Open · kit 14The Lamb can't walk the intro on the Vita yet: ~50–60 MB of game memory shortstill ~45–61 MB short after kit 13 (emulator)Memory · found kit 4

    The intro's one-frame load needs more game memory than the Vita gives. Every kit since kit 9 took memory back, but it is still about 50–60 MB short on the emulator at the Vita's memory layout, so New Game stops at the opening quotes on purpose.

  • Open · kit 16The cult base needs ~54 MB more game memory than the introkit 13: the base needs ~27 MB less (Mac)Memory · found 4 Nov (Mac)

    Measured on the Mac at the Vita's object sizes: Main Menu ~78 MB, intro ~173 MB, base ~227 MB (80,002 objects, only 28,557 of them switched on). Packing switched-off objects until something touches them is the biggest lever. With 100 followers (5 Nov, Mac): each follower is ~201 objects (only ~38 switched on), and 99 more followers added ~37 MB at the Vita's sizes (their setup stopped early on Shepherd, so the real figure is likely higher): a 100-follower base needs roughly 265–275 MB against the intro's ~173.

  • Open · kit 14With kit 12's defaults, running out of memory mid-walk ends in an internal error, not a clean red endunchanged in kit 13 (emulator)Stability · found kit 12 (Mac)

    Seen only on the emulator at the Vita's memory layout, on a route the kit doesn't run (the walk).

  • Open · off by defaultKit 11's exact GPU rows were slower on the Vitacause foundSpeed · found kit 11

    A maths call in one of them ran in the slow fallback mode on every draw (about 16 µs each); the rows stay off. Kit 12 added a check that catches this kind of slow call.

  • Open · decision pendingThe two game-AOT apps have 1 MB less free memoryMemory · found kit 12

    Kit 12's new code pushed their eboot over a 1 MB step; their menus use 3–6 MB less than in kit 11, so they still come out ahead.

  • Open · later kitDodge tutorial turns the whole screen black for 3.5 s; the pause menu's blur draws blackLooks · found kit 6 (Mac)

    Both need the game's own screen effects. Gameplay screens: the Vita doesn't reach them yet.

  • Open · kit 21Main Menu crowd: the PC's grain across the figures is missing on the VitaColours right, pattern cleaner than the PCLooks · found kit 13.1 (6 Nov run)

    On the PC the crowd's bodies have the diagonal mint hatch broken up by fine grain, and their edges are noisy. The Vita has the same colours and the same hatch, but cleaner lines, with grain only at the tips, and a little more mint (25 % against 19 % on a near hand). Next: render the Mac at the PC's 1920x1080 and compare: if it matches, the grain is lost to the Vita's lower resolution; if not, it is a bug to fix. The picture check missed this because it compares colours averaged over blocks: a pattern check and a movement check get added.

  • Open · kit 21Settings: the panel is solid dark; on the PC the Main Menu shows dimly through itSame on the Mac: reproducibleLooks · found kit 13.1 (owner, 6 Nov, PC check)

    Kit 13 brought the Settings panel's background back, but it draws solid (about 25,25,25). The PC's panel is a dark see-through layer: the logo, the Lamb, the pentagram and the store panels stay faintly visible behind the rows. It shows on the Mac too, so the Mac can be used to work it out. Likely the panel's own shader (UI/Watercolor-Background) or how its alpha is drawn: not looked into yet. Keeping the menu drawn behind Settings may cost frame time there.

  • Open · kit 21Settings transition: the PC's black ink wipe is drawn as a plain straight wallTo check with the solid Settings panelLooks · found kit 13.1 (owner, 6 Nov, PC check)

    When Settings opens or closes, the PC sweeps a black goopy ink edge across the screen, with blobs and drips around it. The Vita shows a plain wall with a straight edge. It may be the same cause as the solid Settings panel (the panel's own shader drawn in a simple form): to check together.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Kit 12's new loading-screen shaders are rebuilt on every start (~2.6 s)fix built in kit 13 (kept in the shader cache), awaiting the VitaBoot & install · found kit 12 (5 Nov run)

    The round-mask program (~0.31 s at boot) and three loading-screen look programs (1.22 s + 0.61 s + 0.45 s when New Game starts) aren't in the saved shader cache or the boot set, so every start compiles them again.

  • Fix planned · kit 13The boot's two cleanups make the boot ~3.6 s slower for little gainfix built in kit 13 (skipped in the boot), awaiting the VitaBoot & install · found kit 12 (5 Nov run)

    Kit 12 cleans up at every scene change, including the boot's two (to the loading scene and to the Main Menu), which free only ~4 MB each. In the A/B starts the Main Menu came up at ~101.8 s with the cleanup and ~98.2 s without. Keeping it for the menu exit and the heavy scene changes, and skipping the boot chain, gives that time back.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Settings: the panel's background is missing (the rows float over the Main Menu) with a yellow tintfix built in kit 13, awaiting the VitaLooks · found kit 12 (5 Nov run, step 7)

    Seen on the Vita in kit 12 and reproduced on the Mac with kit 12's Vita settings. Switching kit 12's round masks off alone brings the dark Settings panel back; the new looks and the Watercolor UI look are not involved. Likely the masked panel picture itself isn't drawn when the game asks for it to show. Until kit 13, an env line SHEPHERD_K12_STENCIL=0 gives the correct Settings look (and the menu's ~2 ms back), without the round masks.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Verify Files confirmation: purple blob bands over a red-orange screenfix built in kit 13, awaiting the VitaLooks · found kit 12 (5 Nov run, step 7)

    The confirmation's torn-paper panels drew as purple blobs. They are masked UI pictures like the Settings panel, so kit 12's round masks are the likely cause; kit 13 confirms it with the Mac route that opens the dialog.

  • Fix planned · kit 17Loaded assets from the previous screen stay in memory after it closes (Main Menu → quotes → intro; base → dungeon)kit 13: the release built, off (it frees little in the intro); base ↔ dungeon nextMemory · found 4 Nov (Mac)

    Garbage gets cleaned up (kit 12), but loaded assets stay: during the opening quotes ~85 MB of objects (Vita sizes) are still alive, about the whole Main Menu's 78 MB, and after the base unloads Shepherd's asset list grows from about 116,000 to 2 million entries (Mac tests, 4–5 Nov). Part is needed next (fonts, text, the HUD), part isn't; letting go of what the next scene won't use frees it right where the intro load runs out of memory. The reason: the game calls Unity's 'unload unused assets' at every scene change, and Shepherd's version only sweeps garbage; it never unloads assets. During the quotes the game can reach ~42 MB, while ~40–45 MB more is held only by Shepherd (Mac, Vita sizes). Measured in the dungeons too (Mac, 5 Nov): the first dungeon floor reached through the base holds 268 MB of objects (Vita sizes); reached without the base, 175 MB. So about 93 MB of every dungeon and boss fight today is the base's leftovers.

  • Fix planned · kit 13The first dungeon's rooms never finish building on Shepherdcause found, fixed on the Mac (kit 13)Gameplay · found 4 Nov (Mac)

    The dungeon scene loads, then stays on 'Summoning…'. Cause (kit 13, Mac): the base's setup never finished (a missing collider shape stopped it), and the base only lets the next loading screen end once its setup finishes, so the dungeon's own 'fade in' was ignored. Behind it, a full-screen white panel the game keeps switched off was drawn anyway. With both fixed the dungeon fades in on the Mac; the Vita confirms in a later kit.

  • Fix planned · kit 17Bishop fights can't start: a lookup for a part inside a switched-off object finds nothingcause foundGameplay · found 5 Nov (Mac)

    Every bishop's intro looks for the boss's animation inside an object that is still switched off at that moment. The PC finds it; Shepherd returns nothing, so the intro stops with an error and the fight never starts. A test-only stand-in got all four bishop fights running on the Mac. The real fix copies Unity's exact search rule, checked against the Unity editor first.

  • Fix planned · kit 17Leshy's and Heket's intros: objects wake up in a different order than on the PCcause foundGameplay · found 5 Nov (Mac)

    When a room switches on, Unity wakes its objects in a fixed order (children first for objects made while playing). Shepherd wakes parents first, so these two intros read an animation that isn't set up yet. The Mac test set the animations up first; kit 14 copies Unity's order exactly.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Followers don't finish setting up at the base on Shepherdcause found, fixed on the Mac (kit 13): the paths were missing only at the Mac’s level 0Gameplay · found 5 Nov (Mac)

    When a follower appears, the game checks it is inside the base's outline (a polygon collider); Shepherd doesn't hand that outline to the game yet ('Path 0 does not exist'), so the follower's setup stops there. The base's own structures hit the same gap.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Game code in video memory adds ~9 s to the bootkit 13: a cached form built, extras X5 / X6 measure itBoot & install · found kit 11

    It frees about 23 MB of game memory, but that video memory is mapped without caching, so reading the code from it is slow. Kit 13 tests a cached mapping.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Main Menu: a teal shape under the Lambfix built in kit 13, awaiting the VitaLooks · found 5 Nov

    It is the game's small shrine sprite at the pentagram's centre. Its shader draws only its outline, coloured from a colour ramp (a black silhouette on the PC). Kit 12 wrote that shader for the loading screen only, so the menu shows the raw teal texture.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Main Menu: the crowd is salmon and hatched instead of pale beige fading to greenfix built in kit 13, awaiting the VitaLooks · found 5 Nov

    The 523 crowd figures use the game's own crowd shader and five floor pieces another one; Shepherd has no version of either yet, so they are drawn with the plain sprite rule.

  • Fix planned · kit 13Settings: a few letters missing in some labelsfix built in kit 13 but off by default: kit 13’s step 3 compares itLooks · found kit 11

    Our text shader turns the font's outline data into pixels with a fixed edge band instead of the game's per-letter scale, so the thinnest strokes at the Vita's resolution draw nothing. A Mac test with a wider band brought every letter back.

  • Fix planned · kit 22The boot loading screen's emblems are still pictures, not movingStills for now; a test that worksLooks · found kit 13

    Kit 13's crossfade shows each emblem as one still picture over the shared video. It works well, but the emblems should be animated (owner, 6 Nov). The recordings the stills came from show no movement inside the emblem's box, yet the game's own emblem shader takes a movement direction, a movement speed and a time value, so our recording may have frozen it. First check what the game really does, then give each emblem its own moving loop, still crossfading.

  • Fix planned · kit 14Main Menu runs slower in kit 13 than kit 12 (14 fps against 24, measured)Buffer fix in kit 14; the native core in kit 15Speed · found kit 13.1 (6 Nov run)

    Kit 13.1's fps test on the Vita: the idle menu at 71.7 ms against 41.4 with every kit 13 change off; render 52 ms against 22. The menu profile puts 28 ms a frame in the world drawing pass, where the crowd is drawn. Kit 14 reuses the crowd's buffers (they are made new 5-6 times a frame); if the time is the crowd's per-pixel shader instead, drawing the crowd once per wobble step comes next. Later kits make the whole menu faster: kit 15's native core takes the Main Menu as its first rewrite, then the JIT (kit 18) and the GPU work (kit 21).

  • Fix planned · kit 14Boot order: Massive Monster should come first, then DevolverSwap the two clipsBoot & install · found kit 13.1 (6 Nov run)

    The PC plays Massive Monster, then Devolver, then the Main Menu. The Vita's launchers play Devolver first, as the owner first asked on 29 Oct. Having checked the PC, he wants the game's own order.

  • Fix planned · kit 14Massive Monster's picture plays in slow motion while its sound is rightCaused by kit 13.1Boot & install · found kit 13.1 (6 Nov run)

    Kit 13.1's sharper clip clears two colour channels of every picture in slow, uncached video memory: up to 52 ms a picture, against the 33 ms a 30 fps clip has. The launcher now takes about 6.8 s longer to finish than in kit 13, so the picture runs at about half speed. The fix keeps the sharp logo and does that step in fast memory, or by a route that does not touch each pixel.

  • Fixed · kit 13Boot sequence plays in the wrong order: the loading screen comes between the two logos, with Devolver's sound over Massive Monsterfix built in kit 13 (the second clip’s decoder), awaiting the VitaBoot & install · found kit 12 (5 Nov run)

    Seen in kit 12's step 2 on the launcher: Devolver logo, then the loading screen, then the Massive Monster logo with Devolver's sound, then black and the Main Menu. Planned: Devolver, Massive Monster, the loading screen, then Press X to Start. The step's log will show what started when. After the Massive Monster logo it went black, then Press X to Start appeared as planned, then the menu. Cause found in the run's logs: the launcher's second video decoder (for the Massive Monster clip) is refused with 'invalid pointer' (0x80620009), so the launcher falls back to kit 11's boot, the game's own splash, which brings its own logo, sound and controller notice. The emulator's stand-in decoder doesn't check pointers, which is why it passed on the Mac.

  • Fixed · kit 13The old controller notice still shows on its own screen after the logosfix built in kit 13 (the boot sequence plays), awaiting the VitaBoot & install · found kit 12 (5 Nov run)

    Same cause as the boot order: the Massive Monster clip's decoder was refused (invalid pointer), so the launcher fell back to the game's own splash, which includes the old controller notice.

  • Fixed · kit 13Press X to Start also starts on the touch screen and the rear touchpadfix built in kit 13 (only Cross, every play app), awaiting the VitaControls · found kit 12 (5 Nov run)

    Only Cross should start the game from the 'Press X to Start' screen (every other button is already ignored), but a tap on the touch screen or the rear touchpad also goes into the menu.

  • Fixed · kit 13Main Menu: the logo is grey instead of red and tealpart 1 built in kit 13 (red / teal), awaiting the VitaLooks · found 5 Nov

    Both logo pictures use the game's own 'LogoDesaturate' shader, which Shepherd has no version of yet, so the plain grey picture shows; the shader is what gives the PC's red and teal. Kit 13's looks track writes it and checks it against a capture of the PC game.

  • Fixed · kit 13Main Menu: the floor pentagram is too big, too low and shifted leftfix built in kit 13 (247 px wide vs the PC’s 246), awaiting the VitaLooks · found kit 6 (again 5 Nov)

    The pentagram is one big flat picture lying on the floor under a 3D camera. The PC's graphics chip spreads its texture with perspective; Shepherd spreads it evenly across each of its two triangles, so it comes out bigger, lower and pushed to one side. A camera-math simulation reproduces both pictures within a few pixels (246 px wide and centred on the PC, 294 px and 30 px left in Shepherd). The same affects every flat picture on the floor in the intro, the base and the dungeons.

  • Fixed · kit 12Loading screen: the pentagram emblems drew as nothing and the rings as white squaresto confirm on the VitaLooks · found kit 11

    Kit 12 draws the rings with the stencil buffer and the emblems with the game's own sprite look (the pentagrams are black lines that need it).

  • Fixed · kit 12Verify Files window looked washed outLooks · found kit 11

    The window's panels use one of the game's own UI shaders; kit 11 tested Shepherd's version of it on the Vita and kit 12 turns it on by default.

  • Fixed · kit 12The CPU Speed row read 444 MHz while the Vita ran at 500to confirm on the VitaSettings · found kit 11

    The clock a plugin sets is not the one the system reports; the rows now go by the clock Shepherd measures.

  • Fixed · kit 12The Main Menu's leftovers stayed in memory after starting a gamegarbage part confirmed on the VitaMemory · found kit 11

    Memory in use grew from 63 to 85 MB while the quote screen loaded on top of the dead menu. Kit 12 cleans up at every scene change, on the black loading frames only. Confirmed on the Vita (kit 12 steps 12–16): leaving the menu, game memory in use drops 75.6–77.5 -> 67.1–67.5 MB on the black frames (8.5–10 MB freed); without the cleanup it waits for the memory cleaner's own later pass. The menu's textures (38.8 MB) stay: that's the asset-unloading issue.

  • Fixed · kit 11 (re-issued)Two probe apps refused to installBoot & install · found kit 11

    Their icons were not 8-bit palette PNGs, which the Vita's installer requires; every kit now checks this.

  • Fixed · kit 9The launchers' logo never drew; the second logo had no memoryBoot & install · found kit 8.1

    The decoder's delayed pictures are collected; the logo plays with its own memory.

  • Fixed · kit 8.1The launcher apps refused to start (error 0x8002D003)Boot & install · found kit 8

    The engine module now binds its two late libraries at first use; the emulator refuses modules the way the Vita does.

  • Fixed · kit 8'Join Discord' did nothing on the VitaMenus · found kit 7

    PC-only links are hidden on the Vita.

  • Fixed · kit 8The device profile reported the wrong model and firmwareTools · found kit 7

    It now reads the real model and firmware instead of the memory card's spoofed values.

  • Fixed · kit 7500 MHz refused, and the app forced 444 over the overclock pluginSpeed · found kit 6

    The app keeps the clock the plugin sets.

  • Fixed · kit 6Main Menu camera leaned to the bottom-left cornerLooks · found kit 6

    The game leans the menu camera toward the mouse; with no mouse, Shepherd's resting pointer sat in the corner. It now rests at the screen centre, the PC's neutral picture.

  • Fixed · kit 6The shader cache never hit: 7–8 s of shader building on every startBoot & install · found kit 5

    The cache's padding failed its own size check; now ~0.5 s instead of ~7.4 s.

  • Fixed · kit 4Settings offered resolutions above the Vita's 960×544Settings · found kit 3

    The Graphics list now offers the Vita's own resolution only.

  • Fixed · kit 3Running out of memory crashed the app instead of ending cleanlyStability · found kit 2

    Memory grew while idle (107 → 124 MB). A memory guard now ends the session cleanly with a red screen.

  • Fixed · kit 2Ran at 333 MHz: nothing raised the clockSpeed · found kit 1

    The apps set the Vita's clocks at start.

Built, not in use

Every feature, test and probe result that exists but isn’t working for the kits yet, why, and the kit that adopts, re-tests or drops it. 20 in all. Tap one for the details.

  • Run it · kit 13 (extras X3 / X4)Frame Pacing settingFeature · built kit 8

    Why it isn’t in use: Built as a Settings row, off since kit 9.

    Next: Kit 13 carries a scripted on / off pair on the menu (frame-time spread); keep or drop after it.

  • Run it · kit 13 (extra X7)Kit 11's exact GPU rows (5 drawing shortcuts)Feature · built kit 11

    Why it isn’t in use: Slower on the Vita in kit 11. The cause was found since: one maths call ran in the slow fallback on every draw (kit 12 fixed the same kind of bug elsewhere). Never re-tested with the fix.

    Next: The slow call fixed; on the emulator the three rows cut the menu’s draws 12 %. Extra X7 runs them on the Vita; on by default only if they cause no harm.

  • Re-test · kit 14Four alternative memory-cleaner setups (split nursery, per-scene nursery, M8, the heap-limit rule)Feature · built kit 9

    Why it isn’t in use: Built as opt-in switches in kit 9, never tried on the Vita.

    Next: Checked on the emulator in kit 13 (New Game passes, same results); the Vita arms are written and run when asked.

  • Re-test · kit 20Menu canvas placement as Unity does it (Camera-mode canvases)Feature · built kit 6

    Why it isn’t in use: Built in kit 6, opt-in since its review (it re-placed the menu every frame the camera moved).

    Next: Not reached in kit 13. The cheaper placement the kit 6 notes describe, then a picture check against the PC.

  • Wire in · kit 19Helper-core prefetch (probe P2)Probe · built kit 11

    Why it isn’t in use: Measured on the Vita: a helper core walking ahead made cold memory walks up to 3.4x faster. Not used anywhere yet.

  • Wire in · kit 14Compressed RAM (probe P5)Probe · built kit 11

    Why it isn’t in use: Measured on the Vita: LZ4 data 1.6–1.9x smaller, unpacked at 117–156 MB/s on three cores, 0 errors. Not wired in.

    Next: Not reached in kit 13; one of the walk’s next levers.

  • Wire in · kit 18The Vita's hidden media cores (probe P6)Probe · built kit 11

    Why it isn’t in use: Measured: they unpack LZ4 correctly at about a quarter of a CPU core, one job at a time. Their check alongside video never finished (the probe's video player failed to start).

    Next: Finish the video check, then hand them background unpacking.

  • Wire in · kit 15Framebuffer fetch (probe P4)Probe · built kit 11

    Why it isn’t in use: Measured: ~1.1 ms per pass cheaper than copying the screen, but 1 step off in 33,408 channels. The exact recipe (the GPU's rounding) was worked out since.

    Next: Build the exact version, then a picture check against today's copy.

  • Owner decision · kit 17 (base ↔ dungeon)Unity's real 'unload unused assets'Half-built · built kit 4

    Why it isn’t in use: Shepherd's version only sweeps garbage; it never unloads assets, so they pile up from screen to screen (~40–45 MB held only by Shepherd during the quotes).

    Next: Built in kit 13, off by default: in the intro the game itself holds what stays (~2 MB freed). The payoff is at base ↔ dungeon, where ~90 MB of the dungeon’s numbers are the base’s leftovers.

  • Finish it · kit 14Smaller object headers (4 bytes instead of 8)Half-built · built kit 9

    Why it isn’t in use: Counted and designed (~7.6 MB on the walk); needs both compilers rebuilt.

    Next: Not built in kit 13 (it changes the runtime, both compilers and every app); ~8 MB back on the walk.

    Next: Built in kit 13 as cached video memory; X5 / X6 compare its boot cost with kit 12’s uncached form on the Vita.
